**וִיבְּמָהּ.** יָסִיר אוֹתָהּ מִהֱיוֹת עוֹד יְבִמְתּוֹ, אֲבָל תִּהְיֶה כְּאִשְׁתּוֹ לְכָל דָּבָר, אֲפִילוּ לְגָרְשָׁהּ בְּגֵט וּלְהַחֲזִירָהּ.


ויבמה, by doing so he removes her status as yevamah from her as she is now his wife. She will be a wife in all respects including the legislation applying to divorcing her and possibly remarrying her.



### Verse 6

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 25:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:6:1)


**וְלֹא יִמָּחֶה שְׁמוֹ מִיִּשְׂרָאֵל.** שֶׁיִּהְיֶה הַוָּלָד נֶחְשָׁב אֵצֶל ה׳ יִתְעַלֶּה קִיּוּם פְּרִיָּה וּרְבִיָּה לַמֵּת, שֶׁהֲרֵי נוֹלַד עַל יְדֵי קִדּוּשָׁיו שֶׁל מֵת, וְאֵין הַיָּבָם צָרִיךְ לְקַדְּשָׁהּ קִדּוּשִׁין אֲחֵרִים. וְזֶה מָאַס אוֹנָן בְּשִׂנְאָתוֹ אֶת אָחִיו, וּבְזֹאת הָיָה עָלָיו קָצֶף.


ולא ימחה שמו מישראל, in that the child born from this union will be considered by G’d as if the deceased had fulfilled the commandment to be fruitful. This is easily understood when we consider that this child is the product of a legal marriage entered into by the deceased husband of his mother. The fact that the deceased’s brother did not have to go through a marriage ceremony with the widow of his brother makes this clear to everyone. This explains why G’d would be angry at the brother who refuses to marry his sister-in-law, as he thereby deprived his deceased brother forever from fulfilling the mitzvah of being fruitful



### Verse 9

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 25:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:9:1)


**כָּכָה יֵעָשֶׂה לָאִישׁ אֲשֶׁר לֹא יִבְנֶה אֶת בֵּית אָחִיו.** רָאוּי הוּא לָזֶה הַבִּזָּיוֹן, מֵאַחַר שֶׁלֹּא חָפֵץ לְהַשְׁלִים בִּנְיַן הַבַּיִת שֶׁהִתְחִיל לִבְנוֹת אָחִיו בְּקִדּוּשָׁיו, כְּאָמְרָם זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה (שבת קיח) ״לֹא קָרָאתִי לְאִשְׁתִּי אֶלָּא בֵּיתִי״.


ככה יעשה לאיש אשר לא יבנה את בית אחיו, he deserves this public embarrassment seeing he had refused to restore the house his deceased brother had begun to build when he betrothed his (now) widow. This reminds us of the statement in Shabbat 118 by Rabbi Yossi who did not refer to his wife as אשתי, “my wife,” but as ביתי, “my house.”



### Verse 11

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 25:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:11:1)


**וְקָרְבָה אֵשֶׁת הָאֶחָד לְהַצִּיל אֶת אִישָׁהּ.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהַיְבָמָה מְצֻוָּה לְבַזּוֹת אֶת אֲחִי בַעְלָהּ עַל שֶׁלֹּא חָס עַל בַּעְלָהּ, לֹא הוּתַּר לְאִשָּׁה לְבַיֵּשׁ אֶת הַנִּלְחָם עִם בַּעְלָהּ.


וקרבה אשת האחד להציל את אישה, [the author, obliquely, established a conceptual link between this paragraph and that preceding it. Ed.] Even though the Torah had not only given permission to the widow in the yevamah legislation to publicly embarrass her brother in law for failing to do his duty by marrying her, but had even commanded her to do so, in this instance, when a wife tries to save her husband’s life by resorting to tactics which embarrass his opponent she is punished for doing so.



### Verse 14

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 25:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:14:1)


**לֹא יִהְיֶה לְךָ בְּבֵיתְךָ אֵיפָה וְאֵיפָה.** אַחַר שֶׁהִזְכִּיר דַּרְכֵי הַשְׁרָאַת שְׁכִינָה בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל, הִזְהִיר שֶׁלֹּא בִּלְבַד שָׂנֵא הָאֵל אֶת עִוּוּת הַדִּין, אֲבָל יִשְׂנָא גַּם כֵּן הַמַּחֲזִיק בְּכֵלִים מוּכָנִים לַעֲשׂוֹת עָוֶל, וְשֶׁצָּרִיךְ לְהַרְחִיק אוֹתָם הַכֵּלִים פֶּן תִּגְעַל נַפְשׁוֹ אוֹתָנוּ, כַּאֲמָרוֹ ״כִּי תוֹעֲבַת ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ כָּל עֹשֵׂה אֵלֶּה״ (דברים כה:טז).


לא יהיה לך בביתך איפה ואיפה, after having mentioned the ways in which the benevolent presence of the Shechinah can be assured for the Jewish people, the Torah reminds you that if G’d hates corrupt justice this is not an isolated instance of what G’d hates in interpersonal relations, something we call “civics,” He even hates it when someone owns and keeps instruments which are designed to be used in a corrupt, deceptive, manner, so that even without putting these instruments to use the owner/keeper has become guilty of violating a negative commandment. G’d’s presence, Shechinah, cannot tolerate neighbours who even think of using such means to deceive others.



### Verse 19

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 25:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/25:19:1)


**תִּמְחֶה אֶת זֵכֶר עֲמָלֵק.** מִשּׁוֹר עַד שֶׂה, מִגָּמָל וְעַד חֲמוֹר, כְּמוֹ שֶׁצִּוָּה לְשָׁאוּל (שמואל א טו:ג). וְזֶה כְּדֵי לְהִנָּקֵם מֵעֲמָלֵק עַל שֶׁהֵעֵז פָּנָיו בָּאֵל יִתְעַלֶּה, כִּמְקַנְּאִים לִכְבוֹדוֹ.


תמחה את זכר עמלק, by killing even their livestock, as King Sha-ul was commanded in Samuel I 15,3. This was intended to avenge the unprovoked attack by Amalek against G’d when, by attacking His people, they demeaned His image, besmirched His honour.



## Chapter 26


### Verse 2

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:2:1)


**מֵרֵאשִׁית כָּל פְּרִי הָאֲדָמָה אֲשֶׁר תָּבִיא מֵאַרְצְךָ.** הַמְשֻׁבָּחִים שֶׁבְּכָל פֵּרוֹתֶיהָ, כְּמוֹ ״וְרֵאשִׁית שְׁמָנִים יִמְשָׁחוּ״ (עמוס ו:ו), ״נְקֻבֵי רֵאשִׁית הַגּוֹיִם״ (עמוס ו:א). וְהַמְשֻׁבָּחִים הֵם שִׁבְעָה מִינִין שֶׁנִּשְׁתַּבְּחָה בָּהֶם אֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל, כְּמוֹ שֶׁבָּא בַּקַּבָּלָה. וְזֶה בֵּאוּר מַה שֶּׁצִּוָּה לְמַעְלָה בְּאָמְרוֹ ״רֵאשִׁית בִּכּוּרֵי אַדְמָתְךָ תָּבִיא״ (שמות כג:יט). וּבֵאֵר שֶׁבְּאָמְרוֹ ״רֵאשִׁית בִּכּוּרֵי אַדְמָתְךָ״ הָיְתָה הַכַּוָּנָה שֶׁתָּבִיא הַבִּכּוּרִים מִמִּבְחַר פֵּרוֹת אַדְמָתְךָ, וְהֵם שִׁבְעַת הַמִּינִים שֶׁנִּשְׁתַּבְּחָה בָּהֶם (דברים ח:ח-ט).


מראשית כל פרי האדמה אשר תביא מארצך, the choicest of each category. [the word ראשית is not understood as “first.” Ed] We find the term ראשית used in this sense in Amos 6,6 וראשית שמנים ימשחו, “they anoint themselves with the choicest oils.” In verse 1 of the same chapter we read נקובי ראשית הגויים, “the ones known as the choicest of the nations. ” These “choicest” fruit are the seven types of produce for which the Land of Israel is famous, and this is why we have been commanded already in Exodus 23,19 to offer samples of these in the Temple. The gift known as ביכורים is not literally the fist ripe produce of each of these seven species, but the ones that grow on the best soil the farmer has so that it is truly also his choicest. The seven categorise of produce have been enumerated in Deut. 8,8-9.



### Verse 3

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:3:1)


**אֶל הַכֹּהֵן אֲשֶׁר יִהְיֶה בַּיָּמִים הָהֵם.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁלֹּא יִהְיֶה גָּדוֹל בְּחָכְמָה, לֹא תֶחְדַּל מִלְּדַבֵּר עִמּוֹ בְּכָבוֹד בְּאָמְרְךָ ״ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ״, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁזֶּה לֹא יֵאָמֵר זוּלָתִי לְאַנְשֵי הַשֵּׁם כְּמוֹ לַמְּלָכִים וְלַנְּבִיאִים. מִכָּל מָקוֹם, בִּהְיוֹתְךָ מֵבִיא אֵלָיו אֶת הַבִּכּוּרִים כְּמֵבִיא דּוֹרוֹן לָאֵל יִתְעַלֶּה שֶׁהוּא בַּעַל הַקַּרְקַע, רָאוּי שֶׁתְּדַבֵּר עִמּוֹ בָּזֶה דֶּרֶךְ כָּבוֹד.


אל הכהן אשר יהיה בימים ההם, even if the priest in the particular generation may not be outstanding in wisdom, you must not therefore treat with less respect than his position warrants. This is why the Torah added the seemingly superfluous words לה' אלוקיך, words usually only used when addressing men of outstanding caliber such as kings and prophets. [If I understand correctly, the author mans that only high ranking people need to be reminded by implication that there is a higher authority than they themselves. Hence the addition of the words לה' אלוקיך after the word הגדתי. Ed.] At any rate, the idea is that seeing that the priest receives the bikkurim on behalf of G’d, it is as if G’d’s agent is addressed by the title reserved for his Master.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:3:2)


**הִגַּדְתִּי הַיּוֹם.** הוֹדַעְתִּי לַכֹּל מִתּוֹךְ מַעֲשַׂי אֵלֶּה, כְּמוֹ ״כִּי הִגַּדְתָּ הַיּוֹם כִּי אַיִן לְךָ שָׂרִים וַעֲבָדִים״ (שמואל ב יט:ז).


הגדתי היום, equivalent to “I have made a public acknowledgment of the great works of the Lord today.” We find similar phrasing in Samuel II 19,7 when Yoav accuses David of having insulted all his ministers and generals by grieving over the death of his rebellious son instead of appreciating the loyalty of his followers who had saved his life under most difficult circumstances. The words there are: כי הגדת היום כי אין לך שרים ועבדים, “(by your excessive grieving) you have made clear that you do not consider that you have any loyal followers.”


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:3:3)


**כִּי בָאתִי אֶל הָאָרֶץ.** שֶׁבָּאתִי מֵאֶרֶץ אַחֶרֶת אֶל זֹאת הָאָרֶץ.


כי באתי אל הארץ, that I have come to this land from another country אשר נשבע ה' לאבותינו לתת לנו, as when G’d had said in Genesis 17,8 “I gave this land to you and to your descendants after you.” Seeing that G’d had sworn this oath at the time, I consider myself though apparently an outsider, a stranger, as if I am a permanent resident thanks to His gift, and this is why I have brought these choice fruit that it is incumbent upon anyone who has received the land as either a gift or even as a tenant to present to the real owner of the land in recognition of His largesse.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:3:4)


**אֲשֶׁר נִשְׁבַּע ה׳ לַאֲבוֹתֵינוּ לָתֶת לָנוּ.** כְּאָמְרוֹ ״וְנָתַתִּי לְךָ וּלְזַרְעֲךָ אַחֲרֶיךָ וְכוּ׳״ (שם). וּלְפִיכָךְ אֲנִי הַגֵּר שֶׁבָּאתִי אֶל הָאָרֶץ לְתוֹשָׁב בְּכֹחַ מַתְּנָתוֹ, הֵבֵאתִי הַבִּכּוּרִים הָרְאוּיִים לְנוֹתֵן קַרְקַע בְּמַתָּנָה אוֹ בַּאֲרִיסוּת.


(4) THAT ADONAI SWORE TO OUR FATHERS TO ASSIGN US. As it says (Gen. 17:8), "I have given to you and your offspring to come." Therefore, I, the stranger, who have come to the land as a resident by way of [Divine] gift, have brought the first fruits to the One who gave the land as a gift or as a sharecropping arrangement.



### Verse 4

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:4:1)


**וְהִנִּיחוֹ לִפְנֵי מִזְבַּח ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ.** לְהַרְאוֹת וּלְהוֹדִיעַ שֶׁהַבִּכּוּרִים לֹא הוּבְאוּ לַכֹּהֵן, אֲבָל הוּבְאוּ אֶל הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ, וְהוּא נְתָנָם לַכֹּהֵן עִם שְׁאָר מַתְּנוֹת כְּהֻנָּה.


והניחו לפני מזבח ה' אלוקיך, to make plain by this gesture that these fruit are not really intended for the priest, but are a present to G’d, Who, in turn, gives them to the priest just as He does with other offerings brought to Him.



### Verse 5

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:5:1)


**אֲרַמִּי אֹבֵד אָבִי.** הִנֵּה אָבִי שֶׁהָיָה יַעֲקֹב הָיָה זְמַן מָה אֲרַמִּי אוֹבֵד, שֶׁלֹּא הָיָה לוֹ בֵּית מוֹשָׁב, וּבְכֵן לֹא הָיָה מוּכָן לְהַעֲמִיד גּוֹי רָאוּי לָרֶשֶׁת אָרֶץ.


ארמי אובד אבי, my father, i.e. Yaakov, who was for a while a wandering lost person without a home of his own, was not at the time able to establish a nation deserving or fit to inherit this land.



### Verse 6

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:6:1)


**וַיְעַנּוּנוּ וַיִּתְּנוּ עָלֵינוּ עֲבֹדָה קָשָׁה.** וְאַחַר שֶׁהָיָה לְגוֹי, לֹא הָיָה רָאוּי לְקַבֵּל מַתָּנָה, שֶׁהָיָה תַּחַת עַבְדוּת, וּמַה שֶּׁקָּנָה עֶבֶד קָנָה רַבּוֹ.


ויענונו ויתנו עלינו עבודה קשה, seeing that once the Jewish people had become a nation they were not legally capable of receiving gifts as the gifts given to slaves automatically belong to the slave’s master.



### Verse 9

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:9:1)


**וַיִּתֶּן לָנוּ אֶת הָאָרֶץ הַזֹּאת.** וְאַחַר שֶׁיָּצָאנוּ מִסִּבְלוֹת מִצְרַיִם לֹא הָיָה לָנוּ שׁוּם חֵלֶק אֶרֶץ לָשֶׁבֶת בּוֹ, וְנָתַן לָנוּ זֹאת שֶׁהִיא אֶרֶץ נִבְחֶרֶת זָבַת חָלָב וּדְבָשׁ.


ויתן לנו את הארץ הזאת, after we had left Egypt as free men we did not have any part of the land to settle in, and He gave us this land which is a superior land flowing with milk and honey.



### Verse 10

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:10:1)


**וְעַתָּה.** מֵאַחַר שֶׁיָּדַעְתִּי מָה רַב טוּבְךָ וְחַסְדְּךָ עָלֵינוּ, שֶׁעֲשִׂיתָנוּ לְגוֹי רָאוּי לָרֶשֶׁת אֶרֶץ, וּלְהוֹצִיאָנוּ מִמִּצְרַיִם בְּאֹפֶן שֶׁנּוּכַל לְקַבֵּל מַתָּנָה שֶׁתִּהְיֶה שֶׁלָּנוּ, וְנָתַתָּ לָּנוּ זֹאת שֶׁהִיא צְבִי לְכָל הָאֲרָצוֹת וּמִבְחַר הַמְּקוֹמוֹת.


ועתה, after I have become thoroughly aware how great and extensive is Your loving kindness demonstrated by how You have made us fit to take possession of the land and to take us out to freedom in a manner enabling us to accept a gift which will be legally ours, and you have given us such a choice land, הנה הבאתי את ראשית פרי האדמה אשר נתת לי ה', I herewith present as a token of my gratitude a gift of the choicest fruit of this land which the Lord has given me.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:10:2)


**הִנֵּה הֵבֵאתִי אֶת רֵאשִׁית פְּרִי הָאֲדָמָה אֲשֶׁר נָתַתָּה לִּי ה׳.** הֵבֵאתִי מִבְחַר פֵּירוֹת אוֹתָהּ הָאָרֶץ שֶׁנָּתַתָּ לִּי, לָתֵת תּוֹדָה לְשִׁמְךָ עָלֶיהָ.


(2) I HAVE BROUGHT THE FIRST FRUITS OF THE SOIL WHICH YOU HAVE GIVEN ME, ADONAI. I have brought the choicest fruits of that land that You have given me, to give thanks to Your name for it.



### Verse 13

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:13:1)


**בִּעַרְתִּי הַקֹּדֶשׁ מִן הַבַּיִת.** בַּחֲטָאֵינוּ וּבַעֲוֹנוֹת אֲבוֹתֵינוּ הוּסְרָה הָעֲבוֹדָה מֵהַבְּכוֹרוֹת, אֲשֶׁר לָהֶם הָיוּ רְאוּיוֹת תְּרוּמוֹת וּמַעַשְׂרוֹת, כְּאָמְרוֹ (יחזקאל כ:כו) ״וָאֲטַמֵּא אוֹתָם בְּמַתְּנוֹתָם בְּהַעֲבִיר כָּל פֶּטֶר רֶחֶם״, וְזֶהוּ וִדּוּי מַעֲשֵׂר שֶׁהִזְכִּירוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה.


הקודש מן הבית, because of our and our fathers’ sins the privilege of performing Temple service was removed from the firstborn, who otherwise would also have been the recipients of all the various tithes. Ezekiel 20,26 refers to this when he writes: ואטמא אותם במתנותם, בהעביר כל פטר רחם , “and I defiled them through their own gifts, when causing each first born to pass;” the prophet calls this line the ווידוי מעשר, the confession represented by the fact that the tithe cannot remain in the home but must be given to Levites who supplanted the family priest, the firstborn as a result of their having participated in the sin of the golden calf. (Maasser Sheyni 5,10)


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:13:2)


**וְגַם נְתַתִּיו לַלֵּוִי.** טַעַם ״גַּם״ כְּטַעַם ״אַף עַל פִּי״, כְּמוֹ ״גַּם הָיִיתִי הַלַּיְלָה לְאִישׁ וְגַם יָלַדְתִּי בָּנִים״ (רות א:יב). יֹאמַר אִם כֵּן: מוֹדֶה אֲנִי כִּי גָדוֹל עֲוֹנִי שֶׁגָּרַמְתִּי לְבַעֵר הַקֹּדֶשׁ מִן הַבַּיִת, וְאַף עַל פִּי שֶׁנְּתַתִּיו לַלֵּוִי וְזוּלָתוֹ כְּמִצְוָתְךָ, אֲנִי מִתְפַּלֵּל שֶׁתַּשְׁקִיף הַשְׁקָפָה לְטוֹבָה בִּמְקוֹם הַהַשְׁקָפָה לְרָעָה הָרְאוּיָה בַּעֲוֹנִי.


וגם נתתיו ללוי, the meaning of the word גם in our context is the same as the words אף על פי, “even so.” We find the word גם used as אף על פי also in Ruth 1,12 when Naomi explains to her daughters-in-law that even if she were to get married immediately she would not be able to produce sons who could become the husbands of her daughters-in-law and thereby keep their deceased husbands’ names alive vicariously (symbolic levirate marriage). We must therefore perceive the farmer’s declaration to mean: “I confess that my sin was great so that as a result I am forced to destroy or remove all sacred things from my house even though I, personally, have discharged my duties by giving the Levite and his companions these tithes at Your command. Having done so, I pray that You will look down benevolently. Seeing that normally the word השקפה is used in Scripture as “looking down to confirm something negative,” the farmer had to add that he means that he has merited a benevolent השקפה as expressed by the plea וברך את עמך את ישראל, “and bless Your people, Israel, etc.”



### Verse 15

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:15:1)


**וּבָרֵךְ אֶת עַמְּךָ וְגוֹ׳ וְאֶת הָאֲדָמָה אֲשֶׁר נָתַתָּ לָנוּ.** בְּאוֹתוֹ הָאֹפֶן אֲשֶׁר נִשְׁבַּעְתָּ לַאֲבוֹתֵינוּ, כְּשֶׁאָמַרְתָּ ״אַעֲלֶה אֶתְכֶם מֵעֳנִי מִצְרַיִם אֶל אֶרֶץ טוֹבָה וּרְחָבָה וְכוּ׳״ (שמות ג:יז).


וברך את עמך...ואת האדמה אשר נתתה לי, in the same manner as You have sworn to our forefathers, when You swore to them: “I will take you up from the poverty in Egypt to a wide good land, etc.” (Exodus 3,17)



### Verse 16

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:16:1)


**הַיּוֹם הַזֶּה.** שֶׁאַתָּה נִכְנָס עִמּוֹ לִבְרִית. הִנֵּה עִנְיַן הַבְּרִית הוּא שֶׁהָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ מְצַוְּךָ לַעֲשׂוֹת אֶת הַחֻקִּים וְהַמִּשְׁפָּטִים לְטוֹב לְךָ, וְאַתָּה מְקַבֵּל עָלֶיךָ לְשָׁמְרָם.


היום הזה, that You entered into the covenant with him. The “covenant” comprised the undertaking by Israel to keep G’d’s statutes as something that would ensure their welfare, something He had not done for any other nation.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:16:2)


**בְּכָל לְבָבְךָ.** שֶׁתַּכִּיר בְּלִי שׁוּם סָפֵק שֶׁרָאוּי לַעֲשׂוֹת רְצוֹנוֹ.


בכל לבבך, you must embrace these laws with all your heart, without mental reservations or doubts about their authenticity. Your sole motivation must be to carry out His will.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:16:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:16:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:16:3)


**וּבְכָל נַפְשֶׁךָ.** שֶׁלֹּא יְנִיא אוֹתְךָ כֹּחַ מִתְאַוֶּה בִּהְיוֹתְךָ מַכִּיר מַעֲלַת מִי שֶׁצִּוָּה וְתוֹעֶלֶת מִצְוֹתָיו, וּבָזֶה.


ובכל נפשך, you must not allow yourself to be distracted, sidetracked, by the evil urge, i.e. temptations, because you are so firm in the belief of the superiority of the Lawgiver.



### Verse 17

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:17:1)


**אֶת ה׳ הֶאֱמַרְתָּ הַיּוֹם.** כְּשֶׁקִּבַּלְתָּ עָלֶיךָ לְהִכָּנֵס בַּבְּרִית בִּשְׁבוּעַת הָאָלָה אֲשֶׁר בָּהּ אָבְדַן כָּל טוֹב גַּשְׁמִי אִם תַּעֲבוֹר עָלָיו, הֶאֱמַרְתָ וְרוֹמַמְתָּ אֶת הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ בָּזֶה, שֶׁיִּהְיֶה יוֹתֵר נִכְבָּד אֶצְלְךָ קִיּוּם רְצוֹנוֹ מִכָּל טוֹב גַּשְׁמִי.


In this respect, את ה' האמרת היום, when you accepted upon yourself to become a party to this sacred oath, which puts at risk every physical goodness on earth in the event that you desecrate this oath האמרת, by doing so you elevated the image of the Lord, Who, as a result, will be even more honoured in your eyes than before you entered into this reciprocal covenant with Him. You have thereby demonstrated that it is more important for you to do His will than to enjoy any of the transient pleasures of this life.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:17:2)


**לִהְיוֹת לְךָ לֵאלֹהִים.** שֶׁיְּהֵא הוּא אֶצְלְךָ הַנִּבְדָּל הַנִּכְבָּד מִכָּל הָעֲצָמִים הַנִּבְדָּלִים, וּמִמֶּנּוּ כָּל הַנְהָגַת וְקִיּוּם עֲנָיֶנֶיךָ בְּלִי שׁוּם אֶמְצָעִי כָּרָאוּי לְנִצְחִי, בְּאֹפֶן שֶׁרָאוּי לְךָ לַעֲבוֹד וּלְהִכָּנַע לוֹ לְבַדּוֹ כָּרָאוּי לְעֶלְיוֹן וּמַנְהִיגְךָ, וּלְהִתְפַּלֵּל אֵלָיו בִּהְיוֹתוֹ הוּא לְבַדּוֹ מַנְהִיגְךָ.


להיות לך לאלוקים, that He will be the most exalted of all disembodied phenomena, powers; you are aware that all that occurs in your life is the result of His guiding fate and history, in your case, without resorting to deputies, agents, any intermediaries who carry out His commands. Seeing that this is so, it would be inconceivable that in your religious rites you were to turn, even only as an auxiliary, to any other phenomenon purporting to be His agent. You bow and humble yourself only before Him, directly.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:17:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:17:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:17:3)


**וְלָלֶכֶת בִּדְרָכָיו.** לְהִדַּמּוֹת לַנִּכְבָּד מִכָּל שְׁאָר הַנִּמְצָאִים.


וללכת בדרכיו, to try and emulate His characteristics to the extent He has seen fit to reveal these to you.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:17:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:17:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:17:4)


**וְלִשְׁמֹעַ בְּקֹלוֹ.** כָּרָאוּי לַעֲבָדָיו.


ולשמוע בקולו, as is fit for His servants.



### Verse 18

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:18:1)


**וַה׳ הֶאֱמִירְךָ הַיּוֹם.** בְּמַה שֶּהוּא נִכְנָס בִּבְרִית עִמְּךָ וְלֹא עָשָה כֵן לְכָל גּוֹי, נָתַן לְךָ מַעֲלָה בָּזֶה.


וה' האמירך היום, by entering into the covenant with you, a distinction He has not accorded to any other nation.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:18:2)


**לִהְיוֹת לוֹ לְעַם סְגֻלָּה.** כְּדֵי שֶׁיַּשִּׂיג בָּכֶם מַה שֶּׁחָפֵץ לְהַשִּׂיג מִן הַמִּין הָאֱנוֹשִׁי, כַּאֲמָרוֹ ״נַעֲשֶׂה אָדָם בְּצַלְמֵנוּ כִּדְמוּתֵנוּ״ (בראשית א:כו).


להיות לו לעם סגולה, so that He may achieve with you what He hoped to achieve with mankind when He created man, saying: “let us make man in our image, etc. (Genesis 1,26).


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:18:3)


**וְלִשְׁמוֹר כָּל מִצְוֺתָיו.** וְנָתַן לְךָ גַּם כֵּן מַעֲלָה בְּמָה שֶׁבָּחַר בְּךָ לִשְׁמוֹר כָּל מִצְוֹתָיו, אֲשֶׁר בָּם תִּמְצָא חֵן בְּעֵינָיו. וּשְׁאָר הָאֻמּוֹת אֶצְלוֹ בִּלְתִּי מוּכָנִים וּרְאוּיִים לָזֶה, כְּאָמְרָם זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה ״גּוֹי שֶׁשָּׁבַת חַיָּב מִיתָה״ (סנהדרין נח:). בְּכֵן אֵינָם רְאוּיִים לִשְׁמוֹר כָּל מִצְוֹתַי, אֶלָּא קְצָתָם בִּלְבַד, שֶׁהֵם מִצְוֹת בְּנֵי נֹחַ.


ולשמור כל מצותיו, He accorded you an additional distinction in having chosen you to observe all of His commandments; by comparison the other nations have not been rated important enough in His eyes to warrant these commandments. Proof is the statement of our sages that if a gentile decides to observe the Sabbath legislation voluntarily as a religious act, he is guilty of death, as ever since the first sin, man had been condemned to labour in the sweat of his brow for a livelihood, and only the Jewish people have been relieved of this decree on one day of the week, i.e. the Sabbath. (compare Sanhedrin 58) All other nations have been given only seven commandments to observe.



### Verse 19

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:19:1)


**וּלְתִתְּךָ עֶלְיוֹן.** לְהָבִין וּלְהוֹרוֹת, כְּאָמְרוֹ ״וְאַתֶּם תִּהְיוּ לִי מַמְלֶכֶת כֹּהֲנִים״ (שמות יט:ו).


ולתתך עליון על כל הגוים, in your ability to understand the meaning of life on earth and to teach it. This is the meaning of Exodus 19,6 “you shall be for Me a kingdom of priests and a holy nation.”


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:19:2)


**לִתְהִלָּה וּלְשֵׁם וּלְתִפְאָרֶת.** לָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ, כְּאָמְרוֹ (ישעיהו מט:ג) ״יִשְׂרָאֵל אֲשֶׁר בְּךָ אֶתְפָּאָר״.


לתהלה ולשם ולתפארת, for the Almighty, as per Isaiah 49,3 “Israel, you in whom I glory.”


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:19:3)


**וּלְהִיּוֹתְךָ עַם קָדוֹשׁ.** נִצְחִי לְחַיֵּי עוֹלָם הַבָּא.


ולהיותך עם קדוש, and your being a holy and eternal nation.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 26:19:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:19:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/26:19:4)


**כַּאֲשֶׁר דִּבֵּר.** בְּאָמְרוֹ ״וְאַתֶּם תִּהְיוּ לִי מַמְלֶכֶת כֹּהֲנִים וְגוֹי קָדוֹשׁ״ (שמות יט:ו).


כאשר דבר, when He said at the time in Exodus 20 “you shall be unto Me a kingdom of priests and a holy nation.”



## Chapter 27


### Verse 1

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 27:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:1:1)


**וַיְצַו מֹשֶׁה וְזִקְנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל.** שִׁתֵּף עִמּוֹ הַזְּקֵנִים, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁהֵם יִהְיוּ בַּמַּעֲמָד בְּעָבְרָם הַיַּרְדֵּן.


ויצא משה וזקני ישראל, G’d co-opted the elders seeing it was they who would stand at Mount Gerizim and Mount Eyval, and not Moses.



### Verse 7

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 27:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:7:1)


**וְשָׂמַחְתָּ לִפְנֵי ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ.** עַל שֶׁתִּכָּנֵס אָז עִמּוֹ לִבְרִית בְּהַר גְּרִזִים וּבְהַר עֵיבָל.


ושמחת לפני ה' אלוקיך, over the privilege of entering a covenant with G’d at Mount Gerizim and Mount Eyval.



### Verse 9

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 27:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:9:1)


**וַיְדַבֵּר מֹשֶׁה וְהַכֹּהֲנִים הַלְוִיִּם.** שִׁתֵּף עִמּוֹ הַכֹּהֲנִים לְהַזְהִיר עַל הָעִיּוּן בַּתּוֹרָה, בִּהְיוֹת מוּטָל עַל הַכֹּהֲנִים לְלַמֵּד דַּעַת אֶת הָעָם, כְּאָמְרוֹ (דברים לג:י) ״יוֹרוּ מִשְׁפָּטֶיךָ לְיַעֲקֹב״.


וידבר משה והכהנים הלוים, Moses co-opted the priests to caution them on the importance of studying the Torah in depth. This was because it was their primary duty to teach the Torah to the people. [service in the Temple occupied only a few days’ a year of each priest’s time. Ed.] (compare Deut. 33,10 יורו משפטיך ליעקב, “they shall teach Your laws to Yaakov.”)


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 27:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:9:2)


**הַסְכֵּת.** צַיֵּר בְּמַחְשַׁבְתְּךָ, כְּמוֹ ״אֵת סִכּוּת מַלְכְּכֶם״ (עמוס ה:כו).


הסכת, illustrate the concepts for you in your mind, as in Amos 5,26 את סכות מלככם, “they even named the idol ‘image’.”


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 27:9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:9:3)


**וּשְׁמַע.** וְהִתְבּוֹנֵן.



### Verse 10

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 27:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:10:1)


**וְשָׁמַעְתָּ בְּקוֹל ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ.** כְּשֶׁתְּצַיֵּר זֶה וְתָבִין, תִּשְׁמַע בְּקוֹלוֹ בְּלִי סָפֵק.


ושמעת בקול ה' אלוקיך, you will hearken to the Lord your G’d as a result of carrying such a vivid image of your duties in your mind.



### Verse 15

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 27:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:15:1)


**אָרוּר הָאִישׁ.** הִנֵּה כָּל הָאֲרוּרִים אָמְרוּ תְּחִלָּה בִּלְשׁוֹן בָּרוּךְ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁבָּא בַּקַּבָּלָה, וְלָזֶה אָמַר ״אֵלֶּה יַעַמְדוּ לְבָרֵךְ אֶת הָעָם״ (דברים כח:יב). אַמְנָם הִזְכִּירָם הַכָּתוּב בִּלְשׁוֹן אָרוּר בִּלְבַד, כִּי עִקַּר הַכַּוָּנָה בְּאֵלּוּ הָאֲרוּרִים הָיָה לְקַלֵּל אֶת הָעוֹבְרִים עַל אֵלֶּה, כְּדֵי שֶׁהֵם לְבַדָּם יִשְׂאוּ עֲוֹנָם וְלֹא יִהְיוּ שְׁאָר הָעָם עֲרֵבִים לָהֶם. וְזֶה כִּי הַחוֹטְאִים הָאֵלֶּה הָיוּ עַל הָרֹב רָאשֵׁי הָעָם, שֶׁלֹּא הָיָה עַל הַהֶדְיוֹטוֹת לִמְחוֹת בְּיָדָם, כְּמוֹ שֶׁהֵעִיד יְחֶזְקֵאל בְּאָמְרוֹ (יחזקאל כב:ו-ז) ״נְשִׂיאֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל אִישׁ לִזְרֹעוֹ הָיוּ בָךְ לְמַעַן שְׁפָךְ דָּם, אָב וָאֵם הֵקַלּוּ בָךְ כוּ׳״. וּבְאוֹתָהּ הַפָּרָשָׁה הִזְכִּיר הַנָּבִיא רֹב אֵלֶּה הָאֲרוּרִים אוֹ כֻלָּם, וְאָמַר שֶׁנַּעֲשׂוּ אֵלּוּ הָעֲוֹנוֹת בִּירוּשָׁלַיִם, לֹא שֶׁהָעִיר כֻּלָּהּ חָטְאָה בָּזֶה, אֲבָל הַנְּשִׂיאִים הֵם חָטְאוּ בְּכָמוֹ אֵלֶּה. אַמְנָם בְּהַזְכִּירוֹ שָׁם עֲוֹנוֹת הַצִּבּוּר, הֶאֱשִׁים אֶת הָעִיר בְּאָמְרוֹ (יחזקאל כב:ח) ״קׇדָשַׁי בָּזִית וְאֶת שַׁבְּתוֹתַי חִלָּלְתָּ״.


ארור האיש, all of the “cursed be” mentioned here had first been intoned as a blessing for all the people refraining from becoming guilty of the sins that are mentioned here. (compare Sotah 32) This is also the reason why the Torah commences the paragraph with the words אלה יעמדו לברך את העם, “these are the people who take up position to bless the people.” The principal reason for these lines is to curse the sinners who violate these commandments, so that they alone will bear the burden of their guilt, the people at large not sharing in that responsibility. The reason is that the sinners referred to were in the main the leaders of the people, so that the ordinary Israelite did not have the power to protest the carrying’s on of their leaders. This thought has been stated clearly by Ezekiel 22,6-7 “every one of the princes of Israel in their midst used his strength for the shedding of blood, etc. etc.” In that same chapter the prophet specifically singled out most of the (12) sins listed in our paragraph here. These obscenities were all carried out in Jerusalem; however, this does not mean that all the inhabitants of the city were guilty of these iniquities, mostly it was the highly placed members of society who were guilty of them. When the sins of the population at large are mentioned, these sins are generally attributed to the city in which these sins have been carried out. Compare verse 8 in that chapter of Ezekiel. [the reference to the “city “ as the place where all this had taken place is found in verse 3 of that chapter. Ed.]



### Verse 25

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 27:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:25:1)


**לוֹקֵחַ שֹׁחַד לְהַכּוֹת.** כְּעִנְיַן דּוֹאֵג הָאֲדוֹמִי (שמואל א כב) וְהַזִּיפִים (שמואל א כג) לִמְצוֹא חֵן בְּעֵינֵי שָׁאוּל אוֹ לְהַרְוִיחַ.


לוקח שוחד להכות, as in the case of Doeg the Edomite (Samuel I, chapter 22), whose accusation was intended to win him favours from the king. Compare also chapter 23 about the Ziphites. Tale-bearing is such a type of interest mentioned in our verse.



### Verse 26

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 27:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/27:26:1)


**אֲשֶׁר לֹא יָקִים אֶת דִּבְרֵי הַתּוֹרָה הַזֹּאת לַעֲשׂוֹת אוֹתָם.** שֶׁלֹּא יְקַיֵּם וְיוֹדֶה שֶׁרָאוּי לַעֲשׂוֹת אֶת כֻּלָּם, אֲבָל תִּהְיֶה אֵיזוֹ מִצְוָה מֵהֶם לְבַטָּלָה אֶצְלוֹ, וְהוּא הֶחָשׁוּד לְדָבָר אֶחָד.


אשר לא יקים את דברי התורה הזאת לעשות אותם, who neither observes nor admits that they are all worth observing. but, who in his arrogance, considers some commandment or commandment of the Torah not worth his while observing. In the Talmud such a person is considered a “heretic because he rejects a single one of G’d’s commandments.”



## Chapter 28


### Verse 2

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 28:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:2:1)


**וּבָאוּ עָלֶיךָ כָּל הַבְּרָכוֹת הָאֵלֶּה וְהִשִּׂיגוּךָ.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁלֹּא תִשְׁתַּדֵּל אַתָּה לְהַשִּׂיגָם.


ובאו עליך כל הברכות האלה והשיגוך. They will catch up with you although you did not even make an effort to “catch up” with them.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 28:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:2:2)


**כִּי תִשְׁמַע בְּקוֹל ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ.** וְזֶה כְּשֶׁתִּהְיֶה תּוֹרָתְךָ קֶבַע וּמְלַאכְתְּךָ עֲרַאי, וּלְפִיכָךְ יַשִּׂיגוּךָ הַבְּרָכוֹת בִּלְתִּי הִשְׁתַּדְּלוּת. וְהִנֵּה הִזְכִּיר בִּרְכוֹת בַּיִת רִאשׁוֹן, וְאָמַר שֶׁיִּזְכּוּ לָהֶן בְּכָל עֵת שֶׁיִּשְׁמְרוּ אֶת הַמִּצְוֹת, עַד אָמְרוֹ ״יְקִימְךָ ה׳ לוֹ לְעַם קָדוֹשׁ״ (דברים כח:ט). וְזֶה הָיָה בִּתְחִלַּת בַּיִת שֵׁנִי, כְּמוֹ שֶׁסִּפְּרוּ זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה עַל בִּרְכוֹת שִׁמְעוֹן הַצַּדִּיק וְהַנִּסִּים שֶׁל בֵּית הַמִּקְדָּשׁ כָּל יָמָיו (יומא כא).


כי תשמע בקול ה' אלוקיך, all of this will occur when you make the pursuit of Torah your principal endeavour, relegating secular activities to something subordinate. In recognition of this approved lifestyle of yours, the blessings G’d bestows on you can obviously not be described as something that you were actively pursuing. The blessings mentioned by Moses here were the ones that were in evidence during the period of the first Temple during the part of that period when the Jewish people did observe the commandments of the Torah, until verse 9 יקימך ה' לו לעם קדוש, a condition which was fulfilled at the beginning of the period of the Second Temple as described by Shimon Hatzaddik in Yuma 21 who reported on the manifestation of miracles in the Second Temple as proof of the fulfillment of that prophecy by Moses.



### Verse 10

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 28:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:10:1)


**וְרָאוּ וְגוֹ׳.** כְּמוֹ שֶׁסִּפְּרוּ זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה עַל אֲלֶכְּסַנְדֶּר שֶׁהִשְׁתַּחֲוָה לְפָנָיו (יומא סט.).


וראו כל עמי הארץ, as our sages told us on folio 69 in Yuma about Alexander the Great prostrating himself in front of that High Priest.



### Verse 11

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 28:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:11:1)


**וְהוֹתִירְךָ ה׳ לְטוֹבָה.** שֶׁתִּהְיֶה הַצְלָחָתְךָ בַּקִּנְיָנִים וְזוּלָתָם יְתֵרָה מֵהַצְלָחַת שְׁאָר הַמַּצְלִיחִים בָּהֶם. וּבְאֵלֶּה הַבְּרָכוֹת שֶׁהָיוּ בְּבַיִת שֵׁנִי סִיֵּם.


והותירך ה' לטובה, meaning that your success will be demonstrably greater and more noticeable than that of other nations.



### Verse 14

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 28:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/28:14:1)


**וְלֹא תָסוּר מִכָּל הַדְּבָרִים אֲשֶׁר אָנֹכִי מְצַוֶּה אֶתְכֶם הַיּוֹם יָמִין וּשְׂמֹאל.** שֶׁלֹּא יְשַׁנּוּ אֶת מִצְוֹת הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ, בִּפְרָט עִנְיַן הַמִּשְׁפָּט. וְלֹא יָמִירוּ אֶת שְׁאָר הַמִּצְוֹת בְּמִנְהֲגֵי הֶדְיוֹט וּמִצְוֹת אֲנָשִׁים מְלֻמָּדָה, כָּל שֶׁכֵּן כְּשֶׁיַּעֲשׂוּ זֶה לִכְבוֹד קַדְמוֹנִים שֶׁהִנְהִיגוּ אוֹתָם הַמִּנְהָגִים, לֹא לִכְבוֹד קוֹנָם וְלֹא לִשְׁמֹר מִצְוֹתָיו. כִּי בָזֶה הָאֹפֶן, אֵין לְךָ ״לָלֶכֶת אַחֲרֵי אֱלֹהִים אֲחֵרִים״ גָּדוֹל מִזֶּה, בְּחִדּוּשׁ דָּת לִכְבוֹד קַדְמוֹנִים אֲשֶׁר נֶחְשְׁבוּ אֱלֹהִים שׁוֹפְטִים בָּאָרֶץ. אָמְנָם הַתּוֹכָחוֹת עַד ״יוֹלֵךְ ה׳ אוֹתְךָ וְאֶת מַלְכְּךָ״ (דברים כח:לו) הָיוּ בְּבַיִת שֵׁנִי בִּימֵי יְוָנִים וְזוּלָתָם, עַד שֶׁצָּרוּ מַלְכֵי בֵית חַשְׁמוֹנַאי זֶה עַל זֶה, וְגָלָה אֶחָד מֵהֶם בְּמִצְוַת פּוֹמְפֵּיאוֹ לְרוֹמָה. וּמִשָּׁם וָהָלְאָה עַד ״וְנִסַּחְתֶּם״ (דברים כח:סג) הָיוּ עַד חֻרְבַּן הַבַּיִת עַל יַד רוֹמִיִּים, וּמִשָּׁם וָהָלְאָה הֵם תוֹכָחוֹת בַּגָּלוּת נִמְשָׁכוֹת אֵלָיו.


Moses concludes the blessings applicable to the period of the Second Temple by warning ולא תסור מכל הדברים אשר אנכי מצוה אתכם היום ימין או שמאל, meaning that the people are not to introduce changes in the commandments of the Torah, and especially not to make any changes in the social legislation, משפטים. Neither are they to introduce variations in the observance of certain commandments which were part of the culture of nations that lived in the land of Canaan, and which had been well entrenched in the entire region. Especially, is this forbidden if by adopting those habits, which, though unrelated to idolatry and of no religious significance, you adopt them in order thereby to display some respect for the culture of the members of those tribes still residing in your midst. In the Torah’s view this would constitute ללכת אחרי אלוהים אחרים לעבדם, that you are on the path to adopt idolatrous practices, eventually even worshipping those idols. However, the admonitions up unto verse 36 i.e. יולך ה' אותך ואת מלכך, refer to periods during the Second Temple, the period of the Seleucids until the Hasmoneans started their uprising to rid the land of the Neo Greeks. Eventually, Aristobulus, a King of the Hasmonean Dynasty, was exiled by the Romans to Rome at the command of Pompey. Starting with this incident up until verse 63 ונסחתם, Moses refers to the period of the destruction of the Second Temple by the Romans. What follows are admonitions dealing with our present exile.

וְהִתְחִיל בְּתוֹכָחוֹת בְּאָמְרוֹ **אִם לֹא תִשְׁמַע בְּקוֹל ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ לִשְׁמֹר לַעֲשׂוֹת אֶת כָּל מִצְוֹתָיו וְחֻקֹּתָיו אֲשֶׁר אָנֹכִי מְצַוְּךָ הַיּוֹם.** וְזֶה בְּלִי סָפֵק כַּאֲשֶׁר הוּמְרָה שְׁמִירַת מִצְוֹת הַתּוֹרָה בְּמִנְהָגִים שׁוֹנִים וְזוּלָתָם, וְהוּסַג אָחוֹר מִשְׁפָּט. וְכֵן אַחַר שֶׁאָמַר ״יוֹלֵךְ ה׳ אוֹתְךָ וְאֶת מַלְכְּךָ״ (דברים כח:לו) שֶׁהוּא הַחֵלֶק הַשֵּׁנִי, חָזַר וְאָמַר ״כִּי לֹא שָׁמַעְתָּ בְּקוֹל ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ לִשְׁמֹר מִצְוֹתָיו וְחֻקֹּתָיו אֲשֶׁר צִוָּךְ״ (דברים כח:מה), כְּלוֹמַר אֲבָל שָׁמַרְתָּ מַה שֶּׁצִּוּוּ אוֹ הִנְהִיגוּ אֲחֵרִים זוּלָתוֹ אֲשֶׁר לֹא כְתוֹרָתוֹ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁסִּפְּרוּ זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה (גיטין נו.) שֶׁלֹּא הָיוּ תּוֹרְמִין וְלֹא מְעַשְּׂרִין, וְהֻצְרְכוּ לְתִקּוּן יוֹחָנָן כֹּהֵן גָּדוֹל וְלִגְזוֹר עַל הַדְּמַאי, וְהָיוּ נוֹעֲלִים דֶּלֶת בִּפְנֵי לוֹוִין מִפְּנֵי הַשְּׁמִטָּה, וְהֻצְרַךְ הִלֵּל לְתַקֵּן פְּרוֹזְבּוּל, וּבָטְלוּ מֵי סוֹטָה וְעֶגְלָה עֲרוּפָה וְסַנְהֶדְרִין מֵרֹב הַפּוֹשְׁעִים בְּסוֹף בַּיִת שֵׁנִי, בְּאֹפֶן שֶׁבָּטֵל כָּל מִשְׁפָּט כְּמוֹ שֶׁסִּפְּרוּ זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה שֶׁגָּבְרוּ בַּעֲלֵי זְרוֹעַ. כְּמוֹ שֶׁהוּא הָעִנְיָן הַיּוֹם עִם כָּל גָּלוּתֵנוּ וְשִׁפְלוּתֵנוּ, שֶׁרַב הַמָּמוֹן בִּפְרָט הַחַיָּב בַּדִּין מוֹאֵס אֶת תּוֹרַת הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ וּמִשְׁפְּטֵי פִיהוּ, וּמִתְאַמֵּץ עַל יְדֵי גּוֹיִם וְעַרְכָּאוֹתֵיהֶם לַעֲוֹת אָדָם בְּרִיבוֹ. וּבְכֵן יֵרְדוּ לַטַּמְיוֹן הֵם וּנְכָסֵיהֶם. וּבְאֵלֶּה הִתְבָּאֲרָה סִבַּת חֻרְבַּן בַּיִת שֵׁנִי וְהִתְמָדַת חָרְבָּנוֹ בְּהִתְמָדַת סִבָּתוֹ.


אם לא תשמע בקול ה' אלוקיך לשמור לעשות את כל מצותיו וחקותיו אשר אנכי מצוך היום, with these words Moses introduces his admonitions, the ones which parallel chapter 26 in Leviticus, but are sounding far more devastating. Israel is described as “backsliding,” having, with the help of Torah legislation, been the frontrunner in civilised countries. Whenever the Torah speaks about the Jewish people abandoning the laws of the Torah, the intention is not to make us think that they slid into anarchy, but worse, they slid into adopting the laws of the prevalent cultures and civilisations. Clearly, this is an even greater affront to G’d the Lawgiver, than merely being passive and not carrying out Torah legislation. Social legislation involving tithes was largely ignored, leading to such great scholars as Hillel, and the High Priest Yochanan decreeing legalistic means of circumventing the legislation so that the people would not be guilty of violating such basic social laws as forgiving overdue debts at the end of the Sh’mittah year. The Rabbis, in a number of instances, were forced to preserve the spirit of the law, sometimes at the expense of what may be termed “the letter of the law.” The discontinuance of the examination by the priest of a woman suspected of marital infidelity, Sotah, is one such example, the Rabbis feeling that when infidelity had become the norm rather than the exception, it would not do to demand a miracle from G’d every time such a situation arose. Moreover, the chances were that the husband’s sexual life was not so free from blemish that he could point a finger at his wife. (Compare Shviit,1,3, Maasser Sheyni 5,15, Sotah 46,48,49) Other practices, such as referring disputes to pagan judges of the nation (Greeks or Romans) occupying Israel at the time, made it almost impossible for the Jewish judiciary, even when meaning to preside over Torah law, to be an effective legal instrument. All of these conditions became a self-fulfilling prophecy in the decline of the Jewish people as a holy nation and led to the destruction of Jerusalem and the end of Jewish independence on a land of their own.

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:9:2)


**רָאשֵׁיכֶם שִׁבְטֵיכֶם זִקְנֵיכֶם וְשֹׁטְרֵיכֶם.** רָאשֵׁיכֶם שֶׁהֵם שִׁבְטֵיכֶם, שִׁבְטֵי הַמּוֹשְׁלִים, וְהֵם הַנְּשִׂיאִים וְהַשָּׂרִים אֲשֶׁר בְּיָדָם שֵׁבֶט הַמּוֹשֵׁל.


ראשיכם שבטיכם זקניכם ושוטריכם, the people Moses describes as ראשיכם, are those who are heads of their tribes, elsewhere referred to as נשיאים, “princes. The term ראשalways includes the people who wield authority. זקניכם, your judges. ושוטריכם, the law enforcers empowered by the judges to enforce their judgments. Each one of these categories of people have the duty to teach the people (within the parameters of their special competence) how much better it is for them to accept Torah law than to defy it or neglect it.

### Verse 10

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:10:1)


**טַפְּכֶם נְשֵׁיכֶם.** שֶׁרָאוּי שֶׁיִּהְיוּ מַעֲשֵׂיהֶם בְּהַסְכָּמוֹת הָאָבוֹת וְהַבְּעָלִים.


טפכם נשיכם, people who normally require the consent of their husbands or fathers to do what they do.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:10:2)


**מֵחֹטֵב עֵצֶיךָ עַד שֹׁאֵב מֵימֶיךָ.** מִן הָרִאשׁוֹן שֶׁבַּחוֹטְבִים עַד הָאַחֲרוֹן שֶׁבַּשּׁוֹאֲבִים, כְּמוֹ ״מֵעוֹלֵל וְעַד יוֹנֵק״ (שמואל א טו:ג), ״מִשּׁוֹר וְעַד שֶׂה״, ״מִגָּמָל וְעַד חֲמוֹר״ (שמואל א טו:ג).


מחוטב עציך עד שואב מימיך, from the leader of the wood choppers to the most lowly of the water carriers. The construction here parallels comparisons as מעולל ועד יונק, “infants as well as sucklings,” (Samuel I 15,3) i.e. the highest ranking within its category to the lowliest ranking. Similar expressions are used when the Torah speaks of משור ועד שה, oxen as well as lambs, meaning the most expensive animals of your livestock to the least expensive. Similarly,מגמל ועד חמור, “from the camels down to the donkeys.”

**לְעׇבְרְךָ בִּבְרִית.** אַתֶּם נִצָּבִים עִם כָּל זֶה הַסֵּדֶר וְהַהַסְכָּמָה, מוּכָנִים לִהְיוֹת עוֹבְרִים בִּבְרִית, וּמִזֶּה נִרְאֶה שֶׁכֻּלְּכֶם חֲפֵצִים לְקַבֵּל עֲלֵיכֶם בְּלֵב שָׁלֵם.

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:11:2)


**אֲשֶׁר ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ כֹּרֵת עִמְּךָ הַיּוֹם לְמַעַן הָקִים אוֹתְךָ וְכוּ׳.** וְהִנֵּה הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ מְכַוֵּן בָּזֶה הַבְּרִית לְהָקִים אוֹתְךָ לוֹ לְעָם, כְּדֵי שֶׁיִּהְיֶה הוּא לְךָ לֵאלֹהִים, וּבָזֶה תִּקְנֶה חַיֵּי עוֹלָם. וּבִהְיוֹת תַּכְלִית זֶה הַבְּרִית כָּל כָּךְ נִכְבָּד, צָרִיךְ שֶׁתִּהְיֶה קַבָּלָתוֹ בְּתַכְלִית הַשְּׁלֵמוּת.


'אשר ה' אלוקיך כורת עמך היום, למען הקים אותך וגו, G’d’s objective in concluding this covenant with you this day is to reaffirm that you are His people and that He is your G’d. By entering into this covenant with Him you acquire eternal life, life beyond the death of your bodies. Seeing that the purpose of the covenant is of such importance, is of so elevated a nature, it is necessary that you embrace it wholeheartedly.



### Verse 14

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:14:1)


**וְאֵת אֲשֶׁר אֵינֶנּוּ פֹּה עִמָּנוּ הַיּוֹם.** וְהֵם הַדּוֹרוֹת הַבָּאִים, וּלְפִיכָךְ צָרִיךְ שֶׁתּוֹדִיעֵם שֶׁמַּתְּנַת הָאָרֶץ וְזוּלָתָהּ הָיְתָה לָכֶם עַל תְּנַאי קִיּוּם הַבְּרִית, וּבְאוֹתוֹ הַתְּנַאי בְּעַצְמוֹ יִירְשׁוּ מִכֶּם.


ואת אשר עמנו פה עמנו היום, a reference to future, as yet unborn generations. You will therefore have to explain to these unborn generations in due course that you yourselves only received this land on the understanding that subsequent generations of Jews would remain loyal to the terms of your acceptance. They will continue to inherit the land from you only on that basis.



### Verse 15

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:15:1)


**כִּי אַתֶּם יְדַעְתֶּם אֵת אֲשֶׁר יָשַׁבְנוּ.** וּלְפִיכָךְ רָאוּי לָחוּשׁ.


כי אתם ידעתם את אשר ישבנו, and this is why you have to be concerned that there should not be among you איש או אשה, neither a man nor a woman who would seduce you to follow the vain pursuits of the nations with whom you will be living in close proximity, and who might be the cause that you would default on the undertakings in this covenant. This is the reason I have insisted that all of you be present at this ceremony, seeing that G’d examines hearts He will know who only pays lip service to this covenant and who is sincere about it. You will not be able to deceive Him.



### Verse 17

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:17:1)


**פֶּן יֵשׁ בָּכֶם אִישׁ אוֹ אִשָּׁה.** פֶּן נִפְתֶּה לִבָּם לְהַבְלֵי הַמּוֹאָבִים בְּשִׁבְתָּם עִמָּהֶם, וְיַחְשְׁבוּ לְבַטֵּל קַבָּלַת הַבְּרִית בִּלְבָבָם. וְלָכֵן אָמַרְתִּי שֶׁכֻּלְּכֶם נִצָּבִים לִפְנֵי ה׳ בּוֹחֵן כְּלָיוֹת וָלֵב, שֶׁלֹּא תוּכְלוּ לְהַטְעוֹתוֹ.


איש או אשה, neither a man nor a woman who would seduce you to follow the vain pursuits of the nations with whom you will be living in close proximity, and who might be the cause that you would default on the undertakings in this covenant. This is the reason I have insisted that all of you be present at this ceremony, seeing that G’d examines hearts He will know who only pays lip service to this covenant and who is sincere about it. You will not be able to deceive Him.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:17:2)


**פֶּן יֵשׁ בָּכֶם שֹׁרֶשׁ פֹּרֶה רֹאשׁ וְלַעֲנָה.** חוֹשֵׁב לְהַדִּיחַ רַבִּים אֶל דֵּעוֹתָיו הַנִּפְסָדוֹת.


פן יש בכם שורש פורה ראש ולענה, a simile for people planning to beguile the multitude to adopt their own idolatrous beliefs.



### Verse 18

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:18:1)


**וְהִתְבָּרֵךְ בִּלְבָבוֹ.** יְקַבֵּל הָאָלָה בְּפִיו וְיִתְבָּרֵךְ בִּלְבָבוֹ לְבַטֵּל אוֹתָהּ.


והתברך בלבבו, while accepting the covenant with his lips, he already resolves in his heart to honour it in the breach, comforting himself, thinking שלום יהיה לי כי בשרירות לבי אלך, “I will be alright even though I will do my own thing.” He considers that seeing that he already planned to breach the covenant at the very moment when he accepted it with his lips, the covenant is not binding upon him.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:18:2)


**לְמַעַן סְפוֹת הָרָוָה אֶת הַצְּמֵאָה.** וְהַטַּעַם שֶׁיְּקַבֵּל הָאָלָה בְּפִיו הוּא כְּדֵי לְהוֹסִיף אֶת נַפְשׁוֹ הָרְוָה וּשְׂבֵעָה בְּכָל תַּאֲווֹתֶיהָ עִם עֲדַת אֵל הַצְּמֵאָה וְנִבְדֶּלֶת מִן הַתַּאֲווֹת הַגַּשְׁמִיּוֹת, כְּדֵי לִשְׂמוֹחַ עִמָּהֶם בְּבִרְכוֹתָם.


The only reason he had mouthed acceptance of the covenant was to become at one and the same time a beneficiary of the overwhelming number of people for whose sake G’d was going to bestow so many blessings on His people. This is the meaning of the words שלום יהיה לי כי בשרירות לבי אלך למען ספות הרוה את הצמאה; he knows full well that the holy community of the Jewish people, his brethren, have attained that status by resisting the lures of the evil urge instead of succumbing to it. This is why they will benefit from G’d’s largesse in such abundant manner. He therefore decided to have the best of both worlds by at least paying lip service to a covenant he knows he will not live by.



### Verse 19

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:19:1)


**לֹא יֹאבֶה ה׳ סְלֹחַ לוֹ.** בְּחַלְּלוֹ שְׁבוּעַת הָאָלָה שֶׁקִּבֵּל בְּפִיו, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁבִּטְּלָהּ בְּלִבּוֹ.


לא יאבה ה' סלוח לו, when he will violate the covenant which he accepted as a sacred oath with his lips, although he had mentally had his reservations at the very time when he confirmed the covenant with his mouth.



### Verse 22

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:22:1)


**גָּפְרִית וָמֶלַח.** כְּמַהְפֵּכַת סְדוֹם אֲשֶׁר הָפַךְ ה׳ (בראשית יט:כה), יַכִּירוּ שֶׁאֵין זֶה מִקְרֶה, אֲבָל אֶצְבַּע אֱלֹהִים, שֶׁהֲרֵי עָשָׂה בָּהּ כְּמוֹ שֶׁעָשָׂה בִּסְדוֹם שֶׁנּוֹדַע שֶׁהוּא הָפַךְ אוֹתָהּ.


'גפרית ומלח....במהפכת סדום אשר הפך ה, the people seeing this know full well that this was not something accidental but that it is a finger of G’d. The comparison is based on Sodom seeing that its destruction had both been predicted to Avraham and confirmed by Lot and his daughters who were survivors of that event.



### Verse 23

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:23:1)


**וְאָמְרוּ כָּל הַגּוֹיִם.** כַּאֲשֶׁר יִגְלוּ בֵּינֵיהֶם.


ואמרו כל הגויים, when they are faced with these incontrovertible facts.



### Verse 25

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:25:1)


**אֱלֹהִים אֲשֶׁר לֹא יְדָעוּם.** שֶׁלֹּא יָדְעוּ מְצִיאוּתָם בְּשׁוּם פָּנִים, וְעָזְבוּ אֱלֹהֵיהֶם שֶׁהוּא אֱלֹהֵי אֱמֶת, שֶׁבָּא מוֹפֵת עַל מְצִיאוּתוֹ וִיכָלְתּוֹ וְהַשְׁגָּחָתוֹ.


אלוהים אשר לא ידעום, they did not even know of such a deity’s existence. For such unproven and almost anonymous deities they abandoned their G’d, the One Who had been proven as true, powerful, through all the miracles He had performed for them.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:25:2)


**וְלֹא חָלַק לָהֶם.** שֶׁיִּרְאוּ שֶׁאֵין שׁוּם מַזָּל מְתַקֵּן עִנְיָנָם.


ולא חלק להם, a deity, be it a horoscope or whatever which He had not assigned to them. The reason G’d had never assigned such a horoscope to them had been to show them that they did not need it, as did the other nations. Moses had mentioned in Deut. 4,19-20 that G’d had assigned such intermediaries to the gentile nations.



### Verse 28

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:28:1)


**הַנִּסְתָּרֹת לַה׳ אֱלֹהֵינוּ.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁאָמַרְתִּי שֶׁאַתֶּם נִצָּבִים כֻּלְּכֶם לִפְנֵי ה׳ שֶׁלֹּא יוּכַל אָדָם לְהַטְעוֹתוֹ וְהוּא יִפָּרַע מִן הַבּוֹגֵד, הִנֵּה זֶה יִהְיֶה בַּנִּסְתָּרוֹת בִּלְבַד.


הנסתרות לה' אלוקינו, even though I have said that all of you are standing in the presence of the Lord whom it is impossible to mislead and deceive, so that He will collect His dues from everyone who owes him, G’d collects directly only from those whom it is not possible for His appointed representatives, i.e. a legally constituted judiciary to bring to justice.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 29:28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/29:28:2)


**וְהַנִּגְלוֹת לָנוּ וּלְבָנֵינוּ עַד עוֹלָם.** אֲבָל בַּנִּגְלוֹת הַדָּבָר מוּטָל עָלֵינוּ וְעַל בָּנֵינוּ, וְרָאוּי לָנוּ לַעֲשׂוֹת אֶל כָּל דִּבְרֵי הַתּוֹרָה הַזֹּאת, לַעֲנוֹשׁ הַחוֹטְאִים וְלַעֲשׂוֹת בָּהֶם מִשְׁפָּט כָּתוּב.


והנגלות לנו ולבננו עד עולם, however, any sins committed publicly must be dealt with by this judiciary, forever, [I suppose the words עד עולם mean that there is no statute of limitations as in gentile countries. Ed.] לעשות את כל דברי התורה הזאת, to avenge G’d‘s having been slighted by the public commission of violations of Torah law, especially those affecting other people directly.



## Chapter 30


### Verse 1

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:1:1)


**וַהֲשֵׁבֹתָ אֶל לְבָבֶךָ.** הִתְבּוֹנֵן בְּחֶלְקֵי הַסּוֹתֵר וְתָשִׁיבֵם אֶל לְבָבְךָ יַחְדָּיו לְהַבְחִין הָאֱמֶת מִן הַשֶּׁקֶר, וּבָזֶה תַּכִּיר כַּמָּה רָחַקְתָּ מִן הָאֵל יִתְעָלֶה בַּדֵּעוֹת וּבַמִּנְהָגִים אֲשֶׁר לֹא כְתוֹרָתוֹ.


והשבות אל לבבך, you will be able to distinguish the truth between apparently contradictory phenomena. When you become the victim of what has been predicted for the sinners, you will realise how far you had strayed from G’d’s Torah, i.e. from G’d Himself.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:1:2)


**בְּכָל הַגּוֹיִם.** בְּעוֹדְךָ בַּגָּלוּת.


בכל הגויים, while you are still in exile.



### Verse 2

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:2:1)


**וְשַׁבְתָּ עַד ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ.** תִּהְיֶה תְּשׁוּבָתְךָ כְּדֵי לַעֲשׂוֹת רְצוֹן קוֹנְךָ בִּלְבַד. וְזוֹ הִיא הַתְּשׁוּבָה שֶׁאָמְרוּ זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה (יומא פו.) שֶׁמַּגַּעַת עַד כִּסֵּא הַכָּבוֹד.


ושבת עד ה' אלוקיך, your repentance will have as its exclusive purpose to henceforth carry out only G’d ‘s will to the extent that He has revealed it. Our sages in Yuma 86 have said of this type of repentance that “it reaches right up to the throne of G’d’s Majesty.”


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:2:2)


**וְשָׁמַעְתָּ בְקֹלוֹ כְּכֹל אֲשֶׁר אָנֹכִי מְצַוְּךָ הַיּוֹם.** לֹא מִצְוַת אֲנָשִׁים מְלֻמָּדָה, כְּמוֹ שֶׁעָשִׂיתָ קֹדֶם לָכֵן.


ושמעת בקולו ככל אשר אנכי מצוך היום, instead of listening (abiding by) man-made laws as you were in the habit of doing.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:2:3)


**אַתָּה וּבָנֶיךָ.** גַּם יְלִידֵי הַדּוֹר יַכִּירוּ זֶה, כְּאָמְרוֹ (ירמיהו לא:לג) ״כִּי כֻלָּם יֵדְעוּ אוֹתִי לְמִקְּטַנָּם וְעַד גְּדוֹלָם״.


אתה ובניך, also the children of this generation will recognise this. Jeremiah 31,33 refers to “everyone knowing Me,” i.e. G’d, adding למקטנם ועד גדולם, “from their early youth till their reaching adulthood.”


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:2:4)


**בְּכָל לְבָבְךָ.** שֶׁלֹּא תִסְתַּפֵּק בָּזֶה.


בכל לבבך, without any reservation.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:2:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:2:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:2:5)


**וּבְכָל נַפְשֶׁךָ.** שֶׁלֹּא תִמְנַע מִזֶּה נֶפֶש הַמִּתְאַוָּה, בְּהַכִּירְךָ גֹּדֶל הָעִנְיָן.


ובכל נפשך, without allowing the “natural,” evil urge inspired temptations to sidetrack you by downplaying the importance of some of G’d’s legislation



### Verse 3

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:3:1)


**וְשָׁב וְקִבֶּצְךָ.** בְּקִבּוּץ גָּלֻיּוֹת.


ושב וקבצך , during the ingathering of the exiles.



### Verse 6

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:6:1)


**וּמָל ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ אֶת לְבָבְךָ וְאֶת לְבַב זַרְעֶךָ לְאַהֲבָה.** יְגַלֶּה עֵינֶיךָ לָסוּר מִכָּל טָעוּת מְעָרְבֵב הַשֵּׂכֶל מִידִיעַת הָאֱמֶת, כְּשֶׁתִּשְׁתַּדֵּל לְדָבְקָה בוֹ בְּאֹפֶן שֶׁתַּכִּיר טוּבוֹ וְתֶאֱהָבֵהוּ בְּהֶכְרֵחַ. וְזֶה יַעֲשֶׂה לְמַעַן חַיֶּיךָ לְעוֹלְמֵי עַד.


ומל ה' אלוקיך את לבבך ואת לבב זרעך, לאהבה, He will open your eyes so as to remove every erroneous conceptions which have prevented you from recognising the theological truth of all aspects of the Jewish religion. Once these “blinds” have been removed from your eyes you will realise that everything G’d has done was out of His love for you, and had to be done למען חייך, in order to ensure that you will be part of eternal life after your body dies.



### Verse 7

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:7:1)


**עַל אוֹיְבֶיךָ וְעַל שׂוֹנְאֶיךָ.** עַל הַקָּמִים עָלֶיךָ וְעַל הַשּׂוֹנְאִים בְּלִבָּבָם, כְּאָמְרוֹ ״כִּי אֶעֱשֶׂה כָלָה בְּכָל הַגּוֹיִם״ (ירמיהו ל:יא).


על אויביך ועל שונאיך, both against those who oppose you openly and attack you, as well as against those who hate you only in their hearts without translating their hatred into hostile action. According to Jeremiah 30,11 G’d will eliminate all those nations.



### Verse 8

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:8:1)


**וְאַתָּה תָשׁוּב.** תָּנוּחַ, כְּמוֹ ״בְּשׁוּבָה וָנַחַת״ (ישעיהו ל:טו), וְזֶה בְּהִגָּלוֹת מָשִׁיחַ צִדְקֵנוּ. וְ״אַתָּה תַּעֲמֹד בְּשׁוּבָה וָנַחַת״, שֶׁלֹּא יוֹסִיף לְהַגְלוֹתְךָ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁבֵּאַר הַנָּבִיא בְּאָמְרוֹ ״אִם תָּשׁוּב יִשְׂרָאֵל אֵלַי תָּשׁוּב וְלֹא תָנוּד״ (ירמיהו ד:א).


ואתה תשוב, not from the root שוב to return, but from the root ישב, i.e. you will sit at ease, undisturbed. Compare Isaiah 30,15 בשובה ונחת. This period will be ushered in by the arrival of the Messiah after the hostile nations have been eliminated. Moses contrasts what will happen to those nations and what will happen to the Jewish people at that time. G’d will never exile you again. All this has been spelled out by Jeremiah 4,1 when he said:אם תשוב ישראל....אלי תשוב....ולא תנוד, “if you return Israel….return to Me, and you do not waver, …nations shall bless themselves by you, etc.”


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:8:2)


**וְעָשִׂיתָ אֶת כָּל מִצְוֺתָיו אֲשֶׁר אָנֹכִי מְצַוְּךָ הַיּוֹם.** שֶׁלֹּא תְחַדֵּשׁ דָּת.


ועשית את כל מצוותיו אשר אנכי מצוך היום, not adopting any new religious rites or philosophies.



### Verse 9

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:9:1)


**וְהוֹתִירְךָ ה׳.** יִתֵּן לְךָ הַצְלָחָה יְתֵרָה מִכָּל הַצְלָחָה הַקּוֹדֶמֶת.


'והותירך ה, He will grant you success beyond any success you have ever enjoyed previously.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:9:2)


**לְטוֹבָה. הַכְּתוּבָה בְּסֵפֶר הַתּוֹרָה הַזֶּה.** כְּאָמְרוֹ ״וְהִתְהַלַּכְתִּי בְּתוֹכְכֶם וְהָיִיתִי לָכֶם לֵאלֹהִים וְאַתֶּם תִּהְיוּ לִי לְעָם״ (ויקרא כו:יב). וְזֶה יַעֲשֶׂה כַּאֲשֶׁר יָשׁוּב לָשׂוּשׂ עָלֶיךָ לְטוֹב כִּי תִשְׁמַע, כְּמוֹ שֶׁשָּׂשׂ עַל אֲבוֹתֶיךָ, לֹא בִּזְכוּת אָבוֹת. וְזֶה מִפְּנֵי שֶׁתִּהְיֶה תְּשׁוּבָתְךָ בְּכָל לֵב וּבְכָל נֶפֶשׁ בְּאֹפֶן שֶׁעֲוֹנוֹת נֶחְשָׁבִים כִּזְכֻיּוֹת, וְתִהְיֶה לְרָצוֹן לְפָנָיו כְּמוֹ שֶׁהָיוּ אֲבוֹתֶיךָ.


לטובה...הכתובה בספר התורה הזה, as He said when He said: והתהלכתי בתוככם והייתי לכם לאלוקים ואתם תהיו לי לעם, “I shall walk freely in your midst and will be your G’d and you will be My people” (Leviticus 26,12) This promise will be fulfilled when כאשר ה' ישוב לשוש עליך...לטוב כי תשמע, as He had once delighted in your forefathers (the patriarchs). This time the good you will experience at G’d’s hands will not be due to the merit of the patriarchs but will be due to the fact that your repentance is so wholehearted and unreserved. Due to your unreserved repentance your former demerits will be converted into merits, seeing that it was, after all, these demerits and what followed that have become the catalyst of your eventual return to G’d. You will enjoy G’d’s goodwill just as did the patriarchs.



### Verse 11

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:11:1)


**כִּי הַמִּצְוָה הַזֹּאת.** וְהַטַּעַם שֶׁאָמַרְתִּי ״וַהֲשֵׁבֹתָ אֶל לְבָבֶךָ בְּכָל הַגּוֹיִם״ שֶׁצָּרִיךְ שֶׁהַתְּשׁוּבָה תִּהְיֶה בְּעוֹדְךָ בַּגָּלוּת לְמַעַן תִּוָּשַׁע, הוּא כִּי אָמְנָם זֹאת הַמִּצְוָה שֶׁל תְּשׁוּבָה אֲשֶׁר אָנֹכִי מְצַוְּךָ הַיּוֹם, כְּאָמְרוֹ בְּכָל חֵטְא בְּפָרָשַׁת וַיִּקְרָא (ויקרא ה:ה) ״וְאָשֵׁם וְאָשְׁמוּ״, שֶׁהָעִנְיָן כֻּלָּם הוּא שֶׁיַּכִּיר הַחוֹטֵא אֶת חֶטְאוֹ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁפֵּרַשׁ אַחַר כָּךְ בְּאָמְרוֹ ״וְהָיָה כִי יֶאְשַׁם לְאַחַת מֵאֵלֶּה וְהִתְוַדָּה אֲשֶׁר חָטָא עָלֶיהָ״, וְכֵן בְּאָמְרוֹ (במדבר ה:ו-ז) ״אִישׁ אוֹ אִשָּׁה כִּי יַעֲשׂוּ מִכָּל חַטֹּאת הָאָדָם וְאָשְׁמָה הַנֶּפֶשׁ הַהִוא וְהִתְוַדּוּ אֶת עֲוֹנָם״.


כי המצוה הזאת, and the reason that I said in verse 1 that the repentance must be wholehearted and among the nations, i.e. while you are still in exile, is so that it can trigger your salvation. For, indeed this commandment, i.e. the one to return to G’d in sincere penitence אשר אנכי מצוך היום, Moses reminds the people that in the list of sacrifices in Leviticus 5,5 et al, where various kinds of sin and guilt offerings are listed as means to expiate for the error committed, the essential refrain is always “והתוודה אשר חטא,” that the guilty person first confesses his mistake, his sin, even the one committed inadvertently. Compare also Numbers 5,6-7)


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:11:2)


**לֹא נִפְלֵאת הִיא מִמְּךָ.** שֶׁתִּצְטָרֵךְ לִנְבִיאִים.


לא נפלאת היא ממך, so that you would need to ask the prophets in order to understand it correctly.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:11:3)


**לֹא רְחוֹקָה הִיא.** שֶׁתִּצְטָרֵךְ לְחַכְמֵי הַדּוֹר הָרְחוֹקִים שֶׁיְּפָרְשׁוּ לְךָ בְּאֹפֶן שֶׁתּוּכַל לַעֲשׂוֹתָהּ, אֲפִלּוּ בְּעוֹדְךָ בַּגָּלוּת. וּבֵאֵר זֶה בְּאָמְרוֹ.


ולא רחוקה היא, so that you have to ask the leading Torah scholars of your generation who live a long way from you to explain how to observe this commandment. You can easily observe this commandment even in the Diaspora where you may not have any Torah scholars nearby.



### Verse 12

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:12:1)


**לֹא בַשָּׁמַיִם הִוא לֵאמֹר.** לֹא יִקְרֶה לְךָ בְּעִנְיַן הַתְּשׁוּבָה שֶׁתִּצְטָרֵךְ בָּהּ לְהַגָּדַת נָבִיא.


לא בשמים היא, in order to repent you do not need to turn to a prophet who represents heaven,



### Verse 13

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:13:1)


**וְלֹא מֵעֵבֶר לַיָּם הִיא.** גַּם כֵּן לֹא תִצְטָרֵךְ בָּהּ לְחַכְמֵי הַדּוֹר הָרְחוֹקִים שֶׁיְּפָרְשׁוּ לָךְ, בְּאֹפֶן שֶׁיִּהְיֶה זֶה אֶפְשָׁר לְךָ בְּחוּץ לָאָרֶץ וְלֹא יִקְשֶׁה עָלֶיךָ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁיִּקְרֶה בְּאֵיזוֹ מִצְוָה שֶׁנָּפַל בָּהּ סָפֵק וְתִצְטָרֵךְ לְפֵרוּשׁ חַכְמֵי הַדּוֹר, אוֹ שֶׁהִיא נִמְנַעַת בְּחוּץ לָאָרֶץ.


ולא מעבר לים היא, neither, when you are in exile, overseas, do you need to travel to the leading Torah scholars across the ocean in order to become a true penitent. True, there are commandments which are difficult to master without expert guidance, repentance, however, is not one of them.



### Verse 14

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:14:1)


**בְּפִיךָ וּבִלְבָבְךָ לַעֲשׂוֹתוֹ.** לְהַכִּיר בִּלְבָבְךָ אֶת חֶטְאֲךָ וְאֶת הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ שֶׁחָטָאתָ לוֹ, וּלְהִתְחָרֵט וּלְהִתְוַדּוֹת עַל זֶה בְּפִיךָ.


בפיך ובלבבך לעשותו, to recognise with your heart the nature of your sins and the One against Whom you have sinned so that you will express your confession and remorse to Him with your heart and with your lips.



### Verse 15

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:15:1)


**אֶת הַחַיִּים.** לָעַד.


את החיים, eternal life, not just life on earth.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:15:2)


**וְאֶת הַטּוֹב.** הֶעָרֵב בְּחַיֵּי שָׁעָה.


ואת הטוב, the pleasant aspects of life on earth.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:15:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:15:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:15:3)


**וְאֶת הַמָּוֶת.** לָעַד.


ואת המות, eternal oblivion.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:15:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:15:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:15:4)


**וְאֶת הָרָע.** צַעַר חַיֵּי שָׁעָה.


ואת הרע, the unpleasant aspects of life on earth.

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:16:1)


**וְחָיִיתָ.** לְעַד.


וחיית, forever;



### Verse 18

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:18:1)


**כִּי אָבֹד תֹּאבֵדוּן.** לָעַד.


כי אבוד תאבדון, for you would perish forever.



### Verse 19

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:19:1)


**וּבָחַרְתָּ בַּחַיִּים.** בְּחַיֵּי עַד.


**so choose life**, you will opt for eternal life


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:19:2)


**לְמַעַן תִּחְיֶה אַתָּה וְזַרְעֶךָ לְאַהֲבָה אֶת ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ.** וְאָמַרְתִּי שֶׁתִּבְחַר בַּחַיִּים לֹא כְּעוֹבֵד עַל מְנָת לְקַבֵּל פְּרָס. אֲבָל אָמַרְתִּי שֶׁתִּבְחַר מַה שֶּׁהוּא חַיִּים בֶּאֱמֶת בִּלְבָד, לְמַעַן תַּחְפֹּץ בְּחַיֵּי שָׁעָה לָזֶה הַתַּכְלִית בִּלְבָד, שֶׁתֶּאֱהַב אֶת ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ בְּהַכִּירְךָ טוּבוֹ עִם רֹב גָּדְלוֹ.


**in order that you may stay alive, you and your seed, to love the Lord your God**, when I said to choose life, I did not mean for the sake of the reward. Rather, you should only choose what is truly life, such that you only desire life on earth for this purpose, that you should love the Lord your God by way of your recognizing His goodness and His great magnificence.



### Verse 20

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:20:1)


**וּלְדָבְקָה בוֹ.** שֶׁתִּהְיֶינָה כָּל פְּעוּלוֹתֶיךָ לִשְׁמוֹ.


ולדבקה בו, meaning that all of your activities during life on earth should be focused on giving glory to His name.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:20:2)


**כִּי הוּא חַיֶּיךָ.** כִּי הַדָּבוּק בּוֹ הוּא סִבַּת הַחַיִּים הַנִּצְחִיִּים.


כי הוא חייך, for your cleaving to Him is your real life, the reason why you will enjoy eternal life;


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 30:20:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:20:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/30:20:3)


**וְאֹרֶךְ יָמֶיךָ לָשֶׁבֶת עַל הָאֲדָמָה.** וּמִזֶּה יִמָּשֵׁךְ גַּם כֵּן אֹרֶךְ יָמֶיךָ לָשֶׁבֶת עַל הָאֲדָמָה בְּחַיֵּי שָׁעָה, אֲשֶׁר בָּם תִּזְכֶּה לְחַיֵּי עַד בְּעִיּוּן וּבְמַעֲשֶׂה, כְּאָמְרָם זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה (אבות ד:טז) ״הַתְקֵן עַצְמְךָ בַּפְּרוֹזְדוֹר כְּדֵי שֶׁתִּכָּנֵס לַטְּרַקְלִין״.


ואורך ימיך לשבת על האדמה, during the transient life on earth, during which through study, serious contemplation of your duties, and your performance of them you will qualify for the life awaiting you in the hereafter. Compare Avot 4,16 where we have been told that life on earth is merely to be viewed as a vestibule to the hereafter, a place in which one makes suitable preparations for eternal life in the Palace.



## Chapter 31


### Verse 1

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 31:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:1:1)


**וַיֵּלֶךְ מֹשֶׁה.** הִתְעוֹרֵר לָזֶה, כְּמוֹ ״וַיֵּלֶךְ אִישׁ מִבֵּית לֵוִי״ (שמות ב:א), ״וַיֵּלֶךְ וַיַּעֲבֹד״ (דברים יז:ג) וְדוֹמֵיהֶם. וְזֶה, שֶׁאַחַר שֶׁהִשְׁלִים עִנְיְנֵי כְּרִיתַת הַבְּרִית, הִתְעוֹרֵר לְנַחֵם אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל עַל מִיתָתוֹ, כְּדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יְעָרְבְּבוּ שִׂמְחַת הַבְּרִית הָרְאוּיָה לָהֶם לְהוֹרוֹת עַל הֱיוֹתָם מְקַבְּלִים הַבְּרִית בְּשִׂמְחָה, עַל דֶּרֶךְ ״יִשְׂמַח יִשְׂרָאֵל בְּעֹשָׂיו״ (תהלים קמט:ב), כְּאָמְרוֹ ״וְזָבַחְתָּ שְׁלָמִים וְאָכַלְתָּ שָּׁם וְשָׂמַחְתָּ לִפְנֵי ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ״ (דברים כז:ז).


וילך משה, he was self-propelled, just as was Amram, Moses’ father who married his aunt Yocheved, daughter of Levi, and almost certainly many years his senior. (Exodus 2,1) Another of many uses of the word וילך meaning that the person described acted of their own initiative is found in Deut. 17,3 where the Jewish idolater described had not been seduced by anyone. After having concluded the matter of the covenant between G’d and this second generation of Israelites many of whom had not been born at the time of the Exodus, Moses now proceeds to comfort the people over his impending death. He does so in order that the joy over G’d having concluded this covenant with them should not be turned into sorrow over the prospect of his impending passing from the scene. Rejoicing over having been found fit to become a party to such a covenant is something natural, the psalmist in Psalms 149,2 speaking of Israel rejoicing with or over its Maker. Being in G’d’s presence, such as here, is always a joyful experience, as is offering sacrifices to Him, as we know from Deut. 27,7.when the occasion was to mark the erecting of the stones after the successful crossing of the river Jordan.



### Verse 2

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 31:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:2:1)


**בֶּן מֵאָה וְעֶשְׂרִים שָׁנָה אָנֹכִי הַיּוֹם.** וְאֵין לְהִתְעַצֵּב עַל מִיתָתִי, שֶׁלֹּא הָיִיתִי רָאוּי לִחְיוֹת עוֹד בַּטֶּבַע.


בן מאה ועשרים שנה אנכי היום, Moses mentions his age to remind the people that there is no reason to be sad that someone of his age has to die. According to any life expectancy and law of nature, he was no longer fit to go on living by natural means.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 31:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:2:2)


**לֹא אוּכַל עוֹד לָצֵאת וְלָבֹא.** וְגַם אִם הָיִיתִי חַי, לֹא הָיִיתִי יָכוֹל לָצֵאת וְלָבֹא לִפְנֵיכֶם בְּזִקְנָתִי.


לא אוכל עוד לצאת ולבא, even if I were still able, I would not be able to function physically as a leader should, such as leading you in he conquest of the Land of Israel as I am suffering from symptoms of old age.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 31:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:2:3)


**וַה׳ אָמַר אֵלַי לֹא תַעֲבֹר.** וְגַם אִם הָיִיתִי יָכוֹל לָצֵאת וְלָבֹא, הִנֵּה ה׳ אָמַר אֵלַי לֹא תַעֲבֹר, וְאִם כֵּן טוֹב לָכֶם שֶׁאָמוּת כְּדֵי שֶׁתּוּכְלוּ לַעֲבֹר.


וה' אמר אלי לא תעבור, furthermore, G’d had said to me a while ago: “you will not cross;” even if I were physically capable of leading you, the fact that G’d told me that I would not cross the Jordan would have made my leadership pointless. You are therefore better off without me so that you will be able to cross.



### Verse 3

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 31:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:3:1)


**ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ הוּא עֹבֵר לְפָנֶיךָ.** וּכְמוֹ כֵן אֵין לָכֶם לְהִתְעַצֵּב עַל מַה שֶּׁתֹּאבַד מִכֶּם הַנְהָגָתִי, כִּי אָמְנָם ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ עֹבֵר לְפָנֶיךָ, וּבְכֵן תִּזְכּוּ לְהַנְהָגָה יוֹתֵר טוֹבָה מֵהַנְהָגָתִי.


ה' אלוקיך הוא עובר לפניך, so that you have no reason to be saddened by my absence. You will enjoy leadership superior to mine, by G’d Himself orchestrating the crossing of the river. 'יהושע הוא עובר לפניך כאשר דבר ה. Although, strictly speaking, Joshua is not he people’s leader in that crossing, he will be the one who nominally will be leading you across the river, just as G’d has said.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 31:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:3:2)


**יְהוֹשֻׁעַ הוּא עֹבֵר לְפָנֶיךָ כַּאֲשֶׁר דִּבֶּר ה׳.** כִּי אָמְנָם יְהוֹשֻׁעַ אֵינוֹ הַמַּנְהִיג, אֲבָל הוּא עֹבֵר לְפָנֶיךָ בְּמִצְוַת הָאֵל יִתְעַלֶּה, שֶׁהוּא הַמַּנְהִיג.


(2) YEHOSHUA IS THE ONE WHO SHALL CROSS BEFORE YOU, AS ADONAI HAS SAID. For, while Yehoshua is not the leader, he will cross before you at the instruction of God, may He be exalted, for He is the leader.



### Verse 6

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 31:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:6:1)


**לֹא יַרְפְּךָ.** כִּי יַחֲזִיק בְּיָדְךָ בְּעֵת הַמִּלְחָמָה.


לא ירפך, for at the hour of battle G’d will lend you additional mental fortitude


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 31:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:6:2)


**וְלֹא יַעַזְבֶךָּ.** אַחֲרֵי כֵן, שֶׁלֹּא יִגְרַע מִמְּךָ עֵינֵי הַשְׁגָּחָתוֹ, וְלֹא יִתְקוֹמְמוּ סְבִיבוֹתֶיךָ.


ולא יעזבך, neither will He abandon you afterwards by decreasing the attention He pays to your affairs. As a result, none of the neighbouring nations will dare attack you.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:7:1)

כי אתה תבא, even though I myself have not merited this.



### Verse 9

###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 31:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:9:1)


**אֶת הַתּוֹרָה הַזֹּאת.** פָּרָשַׁת הַמֶּלֶךְ שֶׁצִּוָּה עַתָּה שֶׁיִּקְרָא בְּהַקְהֵל.


את התורה הזאת, a reference to the final commandment in the Torah, Hakhel, for the king to read the Torah once in seven years before the entire Jewish people, minors and babies in arms included.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 31:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:9:2)


**וַיִּתְּנָהּ אֶל הַכֹּהֲנִים בְּנֵי לֵוִי.** שֶׁמִּיָּדָם יְקַבְּלֶנָּה הַמֶּלֶךְ הַקּוֹרֵא, כְּאוֹתָהּ שֶׁשָּׁנִינוּ הַסְּגָן נוֹתְנוֹ לְכֹהֵן גָּדוֹל וְכֹהֵן גָּדוֹל לַמֶּלֶךְ (סוטה מא).


ויתנה אל הכהנים בני לוי, from whose hands the King who would read it in public would receive it. The Talmud Sotah 41 describes that the Torah scroll passed from the deputy High Priest’s hands to those of the High Priest himself, who in turn handed it to the King.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 31:9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:9:3)


**הַנֹּשְׂאִים אֶת אֲרוֹן.** שֶׁנָּשְׂאוּ בְּעֵת הִתְחַדֵּשׁ נֵס בּוֹ.


הנושאים את ארון, one of the three occasions when the priests rather than the ordinary Levites carried the Ark; at the time when the miracle of the waters being split would occur once more, the priests carrying the Ark were the catalyst.


###### Sforno on Deuteronomy 31:9:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:9:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Deuteronomy/r/31:9:4)


**וְאֶל כָּל זִקְנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל.** שֶׁמִּיָּדָם יְקַבְּלוּ הַכֹּהֲנִים אֶת הַתּוֹרָה בְּהַקְהֵל, כְּאוֹתָהּ שֶׁשָּׁנִינוּ (סוטה מא) חַזַּן הַכְּנֶסֶת נוֹתֵן לְרֹאשׁ הַכְּנֶסֶת וְרֹאשׁ הַכְּנֶסֶת לַסְּגָן.


ואל כל זקני ישראל, from whom the priests had received the Torah scroll on that occasion. On the folio in Sotah 41 mentioned earlier, the details of this whole procedure are described.
